484. MARIA DUTTON (32) and MARGARET WEST (16) were indicted for unlawfully uttering counterfeit coin: to which
DUTTON PLEADED GUILTY .— Confined One year.
MESSRS. POLAND and THOMPSON conducted the Prosecution.
SARAH BARKER . I am bar maid at the Gibraltar Tavern, St. George's, Southwark. On Wednesday, 24th, Feb., Dutton came for some beer, and gave me a bad 6 d.; I broke it and told her it was bad, she said, "Is it? I was not aware of that; it's very hard, I have just been paid it for work"—Isaw a half crown, a halfpenny, and a farthing in her hand, she threw down the half crown, I gave her change, and returned her the pieces of the 6 d., but she left them with me; these ( produced) are them.
FREDERICK HILL . The last witness is my bar maid—when Dutton left the shop, I watched her across the road, and saw her shake her head at somebody—Isaw West on the opposite side, she could have seen it—Dutton crossed over to Mr. Bfitterfield's, the Fountain, West followed, and stooped down on the cellar flap, then rose, went to the next turning, and stayed there till West joined her—I left my own premises by the side door, and followed them; they walked together as far as the Blind School, Dutton then crossed the road, and entered the St. George's tavern, I went in by the private door, and saw her tender a supposed 6 d. for half a pint of beer; the bar maid detected it being counterfeit, I sprang round the counter and seized her and the coin, and gave her to a policeman—I sent another officer in private clothes in pursuit of West, she was walking gently along on the opposite side of the way; I seized her right hand, the constable her left, and from her right hand the constable took a counterfeit 6 d.—Sergeant Rudd came up, seized her other hand, and took from it fourteen counterfeit sixpences in a small bag; she had been shuffling her left hand in her bosom before that—we
returted to the Fountain, and I went with Mr. Butterfield, the proprietor, into a cellar, and under the flap I saw this piece of the counterfeit 6 d. tendered at my house and broken, I picked them up and took them to the police court—I got a bad 6 d. from the bar maid at St. George's Tavern, and gave it to Hornsby.
MARY JANE BONNER . I am barmaid at St. George's Tavern. On Wednesday,24th Feb., Dutton came in for half a pint of porter—I served her; she tendered a bad sixpence—Mr. Hill came in at the side door, and I bent the sixpence with my teeth, tod told Dutton it was bad—Mr. Hill took her by the shoulder, and I put the sixpence on a sideboard, and afterwards gave it to Hornsley.
JAMES HORNSBY ( Policeman, L 193). I was sent for from St. George's Tavern, and Dutton was given into my custody—I found two shillings and a halfpenny, in good money, in her hand—I told her she was charged With uttering a bad sixpence—she said that "she was not aware it was bad"—I received from Mr. Hill two bad sixpences, one of which was broken—I saw 7 1/4 d. in good money, found on Dutton at the station.
WILLIAM PALMER ( Policeman, L 125). On Wednesday, 24th Feb., I was called by Mr. Hill, and took West—I took from her left hand sixpence—goingalong, she was rifling her hand in her bosom very much—the sergeant came up, and took a black bag from her right hand—I asked her where she got it from; she said that Dutton gave her 2 1/2 d., to mind the bag for her at the Elephant and Castle.
GEORGE RUDD ( Policeman, L 9). I assisted in taking West, who was struggling with Palmer—Mr. Hill took hold of her left hand, and took this bag from it, containing fourteen counterfeit sixpences; eight were sealed up in a piece of paper with this postage stamp, and the others in a paper which was broken opean at one end—she said nothing—I took a basket from her, containing a penny leaf, two ounces of Epsom salts, two German sausages, and two black puddings.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . These two sixpences are counterfeit—in this parcel of fourteen, here are seven of 1846, all of one mould, and from the same mould as one of those uttered by Dutton, and eight of 1856, from one mould, and from the same mould as the other uttered by Dutton.
West's Defence. The bad money was given to me by Dutton.
WEST — GUILTY .**— Confined Two Years.
[trialAccount t18580405-485]
485. ELIZA ORCHARD (25) was indicted for a like offence.
MR. POLAND conducted the Prosecution.
GEORGE SPENCE . I am shopman to Mr. Jennings, a butcher, of 22, White Street, Borough. On 12th Feb., about 9 o'clock at night, the prisoner came for half a pound of sausages, which came to 2 d., she gave me one shilling, I gave her tenpence, and she left—I put the shilling with some coppers, there was no other shilling there—I afterwards found it was bad—I showed it to my master next day; it was put on the parlour mantle shelf, and remained there all night—the prisoner came again next day, and I served her with one pound and a half of bullock's cheek, which came to 4 1/2 d.—she gave me a shilling; I bent it with my teeth and told her it was bad—she offered me a good one, as I suppose, but I told her she had given me one on Wednesday—shesaid she had never been in the shop before—I gave her in charge with the two coins.
WILLIAM JAMES JENNINGS . I am a butcher, and keep an oyster shop at
22, White Street, Borough. Spence took the shilling off the mantlepiece and showed it to me; I found it to be bad, and put it down again.
JAMES GODLEY ( Policeman, M 258). I took the prisoner; she denied being there on the 12th—I produce the shilling.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . These shillings are bad.
GUILTY .— Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18580405-486]
486. ELIZABETH ADAMS (22) was indicted for a like offence.
MESSRS. THOMPSON and LAXTON conducted the Prosecution.
JOSHUA WILMSHURST . I am a brush manufacturer, of 3, St. Ann's Street, Borough. On 23d Feb., a little before 7 o'clock in the evening, the prisoner came for a hearth brush, I sold her one at 4 1/2 d.—she gave me a florin; I told her I did not like its appearance, it was too smooth—she said I might depend upon it it was good silver; she wished she had 100 of them—and said, "Have you another to conpare them?"—I took another florin out of the till, which I had taken of a female five or ten minutes previous, compared them, sounded them, and found them exactly alike, but I had omitted to mark the one she gave me; so I laid the two florins in the till among the halfpence, gave her change, and she left the shop—I afterwards found that they were both bad—I took them to the station and gave them to Browning—I saw the prisoner next morning when she was locked up, and have no doubt she is the same person.
Q. Did you not say before the Magistrate that there were three florins in your till? A. Yes; there was a good one at the bottom of the till—when I saw you at the extreme end of the cell; it was very dark, and I said, "I do not think this is her; this person is older;" but when you got to the broad daylight, I said, "By God! that is the woman."
CORNELIUS BREMERKAMP . I am a basket maker, of 28, Union Street, Borough. On Monday, 22d Feb., I sold the prisoner a basket for 1 1/2 d.; she gave me a shilling; I gave her the change, and after she was gone I found it Was bad; I kept it separate—on 23d Feb., she came again for a basket, which came to 4 d., and gave me a bad florin—I detected it immediately, and gave her in custody, with both the coins.
JOHN BROWNING ( Policeman, A 505). The prisoner was given into my charge. I received this shilling and florin from Bremerkamp, and these two florins from Wilmshurst.
MR. WEBSTER. These are all bad; the florins are all three from one mould.
GUILTY .**— Confined Eighteen Months.
[trialAccount t18580405-487]
487. JOHN RYAN (17) was indicted for a like offence; to which he
PLEADED GUILTY *— Confined Twelve Months.
[trialAccount t18580405-488]
488. SARAH JOHNSON (30) was indicted for a like offence.
MESSRS. THOMPSON and LAXTON conducted the Prosecution.
WILLIAM HELLWELL . I am a fishmonger, of Gloucester Road, Walworth. The prisoner came to my shop on 28th Jan., about half past 10 o'clock at night, for a cooked fish, and gave me a bad crown—I asked her where she got it; she said she was unfortunate, and took it from a policeman—she went away, but I followed her, and gave her into custody to Woolnough, with the crown, which I marked.
WILLIAM WOOLNOUGH ( Policeman, P 109). I took the prisoner from Hellwell—he gave me this crown—the prisoner gave her name Mary Ann
Bower, 26 South Street, Blackfriars Road—there is no such street—she was remanded till 3d Feb., and then discharged, on account of there being only one piece—I have lost the coin; I am quite sure it was bad.
THOMAS RANDALL . I am a baker, of No. 3, Agnes Street, Waterloo Road. On Thursday, 6th March, about 8 o'clock in the evening, the prisoner came for two half quartern loaves—she gave me a bad crown—I told her it was bad—she said she did not know it; it was given to her by a gentleman on Blackfriars bridge—I gave her in charge to Day, with the crown, having marked it.
JESSE DAY ( Policeman, L 168). I took the prisoner at Mr. Randall's shop, and received the crown—she said that a gentleman gave it to her in Blackfriars Road—I asked her name; she said, "Sarah Johnson," and that she lived in Lambeth, but had no home.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . This crown is bad.
GUILTY .— Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18580405-489]
489. MARIA SMITH was indicted for a like offence.
MESSRS. THOMPSON and LAXTON conducted the Prosecution.
EMILY SMITH . My father is a corn chandler at Knight's Place, Wandsworth. On 2d March, about 12 o'clock in the day, the prisoner came for a pint of beans; it came to 2 1/4 d.—she gave me a half crown; it bent very easily, and I told her I thought it was bad, and she had better take it back—shesaid she should not take it back, her husband must take it back, as he had taken it from his master this morning—I gave it to my father.
GEORGE SMITH . My daughter gave me this coin ( produced)—I fold the prisoner it was not good, and asked her where she got it—she said her husband took it for his work in the market—I asked her name; she said, "Neale," or "O'Neale," and pretended to cry; I do not know whether it was real—I sent her to fetch her husband, and she went away—I followed her to the soup kitchen, Vauxhall Wharf, and called a policeman—She said, "Do forgive me, I know I have done wrong; do not take me to the station"—I said, "Where did you get it?" she said, "I dare not tell you"—I said, "Where do you live?" she said, "In the Mint"—on the road, she said that she was hungry and dry, and a gentleman gave it to her to get something—Isaid, "It is strange that you should go to a corn chandler's shop to get peas,"and that if she had gone tcr a baker's, it would have been more feasible—she said that she did not know what she was doing.
Cross-examined by MR. PAYNE. Q. When she said, "Forgive me," did not she say, "I did not know it was a bad one; I had it given to me this morning?" A. I will not swear she did not.
HENRY MEEKING ( Policeman, L 143). Mr. Smith pointed out the prisoner to me, and I followed her from Vauxhall Wharf, and overtook her in Tyer Street—I said, "Where did you get the half crown?"—she said that some one gave it to her—I asked her who; she said she durst not tell me—I asked her what made her so frightened, when she was told that it was bad—she said, "Because I knew I was doing wrong"—she refused her name and address—Isaid, "You must have known it was bad"—she said, "No; a man gave it to me opposite"—she afterwards srtid that she lived in Lambeth Walk—I said, "I know you do not live anywhere over there"—she said she dare not tell me where she lived—she aftehwards said that she lived in the, Borough, in lodging houses, whenever she got 3 d. to pay for a lodging, and that she had taken the half crown for a bad purpose—this was all in answer to questions
of mine.
Cross-examined. Q. Was not she crying? A. She began to cry afterwards.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . This half crown is counterfeit.
NOT GUILTY .
[trialAccount t18580405-490]
490. DANIEL HAYES (28) was indicted for a like offence.
MR. THOMPSON conducted the Prosecution.
SUSAN DOUTHWAL . On Thursday, 11th March, the prisoner came into my mother's shop, between 3 aod 4 o'clock in the evening, and had some bread and butter, tea and eggs; he gave me a good shilling, and I gave him the change—he came again in about half an hour, and had some cofee and bread and butter, he gave me a florin; I gave him 1 s. 6 1/2 d., and put the florin in my pocket, where I had no silver—the prisoner went out, and after he had gone I went to give change, and discovered that it was bad—he came in again between 3 and 4 o'clock in the morning, for a cup of coffee, and gave me a shilling, it was bad, and I gave it back to him (I had not then discovered that the florin was bad)—he said, "I will give you in charge, for you gave it to me"—he walked across the road wilh me, and said that he would rather be the looser of the shilling than be given in charge—I gave him in charge with the florin, and he gave the inspector the shilling; I marked them both, these are them.
Prisoner. Q. Where did you put the shilling? A. In my pocket, and When I went into the room I gave it to my aunt—you came in with some female.
JAMES M'CARTHY ( Policeman, M 134). On 11th March, the last witness gave the prisoner into my custody—he had a shilling in his hand, he attempted to run away, but I caught him; he resisted, very much—I asked him for the shilling at the station, he would not give it to me, but afterwards gave it up to the inspector in my presence—I receieed this florin from the prosecutrifx; she said, "He has got the counterfeit shilling in his hand."
Prisoner. Q. Was I going toward the station, or from it? A. I cannot say; I met you half way down Suffolk Street—you did not say that you were going to the station to get satisfaction for a bad shilling.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . This florin and shilling are both, counterfeit.
Prisoner's Defence. Does it stand to, reason that I would take a had shilling to the station to get myself into trouble?
The Prisoner called
MARY ANN SWEENEY . I went with the prisoner twice to the coffee house in Suffolk Street; first at half past 12 o'clock at night, and again between 3 and 4 o'clock—he asked me what I would have; I said, "A cup of tea and a slice of bread and butter," and he called for a cup of coffee—he asked her whether she had a room; she said, "Yes"—he asked the price, and she said 1 s. 6 d.—heoffered 1 s., and she said, "As we are not busy, you shall have it for 1 s.;" she tried the shilling twice before putting it into her pocket, and then went into the kitchen to get a candle—I said that I would not go up stairs till I had had a cup of tea, and he said then be would not stop at all—he asked for his money back; she turned to a person in the kitchen, and said, "Shall I give it to him back?"—she said, "Do not have any dispute about it,"when she gave it to him, he said, "This is a bad shilling"—she said, "It is the one you gave me"—he said, "No; I gave you a new, Victoria shilling"—shesaid that she had no other—only one young chap was present, sitting at the bar—I had no refreshment at all—two men called for something when we went in first, and gave her a 2 s. piece.
SUSAN DOUTHWAL re-examined. There is not one word of that true.
GUILTY .*— Confined Two Years.
[trialAccount t18580405-491]
491. THOMAS BERGANCE (27), CHARLES SMITH (22), and JULIA SMITH (17) , Unlawfully uttering counterfeit coin, having other counterfeit coin in their possession.
MESSRS. POLAND, THOMPSON, and LAXTON conducted the Prosecution.
RICHARD THOMAS WEEKS . I keep the Oak, in High Street, Norwood. On 24th March, the prisoners came, and had a pint of porter—Berganoe paid me with a penny and two halfpence; they stayed some little time, conversing and drinking—I am sure they are the parties.
Bergance. Q. What time was it? A. About 6 o'clock—I had never seen you before.
JOHN BARBER . I am a labourer. I saw Berganece come in, followed by the others; they had a pint of porter, and drank and talked together.
SELINA HALL . I keep the Robin Hood, at Lower Norwood. On 24th March, Bergance came, between 7 and 8 o'clock, and called for half a pint of porter; he tendered a shilling; I bent it, put told him it was bad—he said, "Is it? I suppose I have taken it this afternoon; I have been at Croydon to see my mother"—he felt in his pockets, and at last he found 1 d., and I put the shilling on the counter for him to take, and he said, "You had better keep that; you may be able to detect somebody else—he went to the door, and said, "What is the next train to London Bridge?"—I said, "There is more than one to London Bridge, and if you make haste you will catch it"—I then went up the hill with James Collins, and saw Bergance standing with his back agatinst the wall—I went into a little shop; when I came out he crossed the road, and joined the two Smiths, who were standing about twenty yards off—they all went up the hill, and I follwed them, and gave information—Ikept the money in my pocket, and gave it to the sergeant.
WILLIAM MARSH ( Policeman, P 33). I was at the police station, Norwood, when the last witness came and made a communication to me—I went out, and found the two Smiths standing with their backs to the railings, about twenty yards from the Windsor Castle, beer shop—I said, "I Want you to go with me to the station; I suspect you are connected with a man in passing bad coin; where is the other man?"—Julia replid, "He is at a beer shop, lower down"—as we went to the station, I saw Julia pass her hand to Charles, but did not see what it was—I searched Charles at the station, and found two bad shillings ( produced) in a piece of blue paper in his waistcoat pocket—I asked him how he accounted for them—Julia said that she picked them up in front of a public house in London—I locked them up, and went to the Windsor Castle, and saw Bergance in front of the bar—I asked the landlady if she had taken any base coin, and when she opened the till to look, Bergance walked out—she found a base shilling, and gave it to me ( produced)—I stopped Bergance about 20 yards from the house, and said "You must come back with me; I suspect you have been passing bad coin;" he said "I will go with you; you need not take hold of me"—I took him to the Windsor Castle, and thence to the station, searched him, and found 31 s. in silver, and 10 d. in copper, all good, and these six new combs, which he said he bought on London Bridge.
HENRY BIGGS . I keep the Windsor Castle. On 24th March, Bergance gave me one shilling for a half pint of porter—I put it in the till, and asked him if he had got smaller change, as I had none in the drawer; he said that he had not; and I sent my little boy out for it—he asked me if there was a train for Waterloo Bridge; I said "No; but there is one for London Bridge, at 2 minutes past 8 o'clock;" he said, "That will do very well"—Igave him his change, sixpence, and threepence in copper—Marsh came
in and asked me if I had any stranger in the house—I said "There is one in the bar, and one in the tap room"—I looked in the till, and discovered a bad shilling, which I gave to Marsh—I cannot identify it—there were two other shillings there.
JAMES WATERS . I live at the Robin Hood, Gipsy Hill. On 24th March, Selina Hall spoke to me, and I saw Bergance between 7 and 8 o'clock, opposite to M'Adams'; he joined the other two, and they all went together to the station.
JOHN GODSELL ( Policeman, P 143). I was at the station when the prisoners were locked up—Berganer called out, "Julia, do not tell them where Letty is; "she said, "No"—they then wished each other good night.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . These four shillings are bad, and from one mould.
Berganer's Defence. I am innocent.
Charles Smith's Defence. My wife picked up the two shillings, and gave them to me; I did not know they were bad.
GUILTY .— Confined Nine Months each.
[trialAccount t18580405-492]
492. MARY HUNT , Unlawfully uttering counterfeit coin.
MR. SCOBELL conducted the Prosecution.
JANE FRIEND . I am the wife of William Friend, a corn chandler, of No. 70, New Palace Road, Southwark. On 17th March, about 2 o'clock, the prisoner came for a pint of split peas—my man served her, and I saw her tender a bad half crown in payment—I put the change down, she took it up in a harried manner, and left; I found it was bad; my husband went after her aud gave her in charge.
WILLIAM FRIEND . I am the husband of the last witness. I Went after the prisoner, and gave her in charge.
GEORGE BARR ( Policeman, L 34). I took the prisoner—she was take to Lambeth police court, and discharged.
ELIZABETH KING . I am the wife of Henry King, of Evelin Street, Deptford, a china dealer. On the 27th March, the prisoner came, between 6 and 7 o'clock in the evening, for two plates, at a 1 d. each, and gave my daughter a half crown—she gave it me, and I found that it was bad—I marked it; and gave ber in charge.
JOHN LOCKER ( Policeman, K 295). I received the prisoner, and this half crown—she said she did not know it was bad; a gentleman gave it to her down the road.
WILLIAM WEBSTER . These half crowns are both bad.
GUILTY .— Confined Six Months.
Before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18580405-493]
493. JAMES HOLLOWAY , Breaking and entering the dwelling house of Timothy M'Carthy, and stealing therein 4 s. 6 d., his moneys.
TIMOTHY M'CARTHY . I keep a beer shop in Providence Street, Rotherhithe, I reside there alone; I padlock the door when I leave, and take the key with me—I go out on Sundays to divine service, and for some Sundays when I came home I have missed money—on 28th March, I missed a half crown, and a florin from my trousers' pocket, in my bed room—I had left home about half past 10 o'clock, and came back about 11 o'clock—I afterwards saw it at the station—the prisoner has been in and out like other customers, and has done several things for me—if I wanted a pint of beer taken out, he would take it, but he was not in my service—this money ( produced) I had marked.
Prisoner. I was in the habit of going in and out of the house, and waiting, for three months, and taking you coffee when you were in bed, and serving, and opening the house.
EDWARD BARKER ( Policeman). In consequence of information, on Sunday,28th March, I concealed myself in the prosecutor's house, when he went out; the prisoner came in and went up stairs, and when he came down, I came out from my hiding place and took him; he had fastened himself in with the padlock—I found the half crown and florin on him—he had 9 s. 1 d. altogether.
Prisoner. He was intoxicated; I have carried him up td bed several times.
GUILTY — The prosecutor stated that he had missed 18 l. 10 s., and a watch, and pair of boots.
Confined Twelve Months
The following case was omitted from the New Court, Wednesday.
[trialAccount t18580405-494]
494. WILLIAM CARDING (27) , Burglariously breaking and entering the dwelling house of Richard Cooke, with intent to steal.
GEORGE ROOTS ( Police sergeant, D 7). At half past 12 o'clock, on the morning of 1st March, I was on duty in Orchard Street—I heard a noise in the Samaritan Hospital—I went back, got over a wall, and examined the premises—I found the place had been broken open—I came back and got assistance, and it the house I found the prisoner, apparently drunk and asleep—Igot him no—I am positive he was sober—I asked him what he was doing there—he said, "Nothing"—I asked him how be got there—he said he did not know—I found marks on the donation box corresponding with this knife which I found on the prisoner.
LOUIS RICHARD COOK . I am house surgeon there. I was called up about half past 12 o'clock—I went down, and saw the prisoner—my impression is, that he was not quite sober; he could not have been very drunk, or he could not have got in—I did not notice any smell of spirits.
JANE MOULTON . I am nurse in the Samaritan Hospital. I saw marks on the passage door on the next morning—it had been forced open—it was all right the night before—the panes of glass were all right on the Sunday night—Iknow the prisoner, his wife was in our service—I had seen the prisoner there.
Prisoner. My wife told you the night previous how I was situated. Witness. She said you were very drunk.
GUILTY .*— Confined Twelve Months.

sentences, and then
ADJOURNED TO MONDAY, MAY 10 TH, 1858.
